# Provident-Electron — Pending / Upstream Constraints

Maintained by the document-archival loop (AGENTS.md item 6). Three kinds of
rows: (a) UPSTREAM — constraints owned by the `provident-ssr` project that this
repo must respect (imported from `../Preempt-Providence/docs/`); (b) DEFERRED —
lower-value adversarial gaps parked until a use case surfaces; (c) SPECULATIVE /
IN GATE — in-flight proposals with their recorded constraints.

Retired PARKED and SPECULATIVE rows are archived in the gitignored `archive/`
dir and do not ship in a fork (see `docs/FORKER.md` §1/§5).

## UPSTREAM (imported constraints)

| Constraint | Source | What this repo does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NEVER edit `node_modules/provident-ssr/` or `../Preempt-Providence/` source | AGENTS.md (this repo) | All gaps recorded in `docs/defects.md` → `docs/HANDOFF.md` |
| Engine is ESM-only; `.js`-suffixed imports | upstream decisions.md NPM-PACKAGING | Renderer bundle imports `provident-ssr` barrel + `provident-ssr/core/*.js`; main is bundled CJS (the SDK's CJS export condition) |
| `on<event>="true"` is inert; synthetic events target the producing graph, never the HTML | upstream ssr-synthetic-event.md §2.3 (DOM-F12) | MCP dispatch drives `Supervisor.dispatchEvent` on the graph; HTML is a view |
| Phase A dispatch never re-renders | upstream handlers.md §3 | The Runtime calls `render()` after each dispatch (REQ-GAP-5) |
| No SSR-only render path / no `data-wire` on SSR output | upstream ssr-synthetic-event.md §3, adapters.md | The SSR re-emit goes through the same `SSRFragmentAdapter`; addressability is host-side |
| `clientConfig` gates + `content` payloads = legacy envelope semantics | upstream translate.md §1 | The demo envelope uses `runInstantiation: true, runRendering: true`, `content: []` |
| Inline handlers default to MODERN; legacy requires `format: 'legacy'` | upstream translate.ts FORMAT MARKER | Demo bodies use modern; the gap is catalogued (REQ-GAP-1) |

## DEFERRED (adversarial gaps — lower value)

| Item | Date | Constraint / revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Journal adversarial gap: `base-boundary` status after condense** (GAP 1) | 2026-08-26 | **PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-08-26.** `maxJournalLength` is now configurable via SecuritySettings; the Supervisor accepts it; the SecurePanels UI has a numeric input for it; the production chain (store → IPC → renderer → Runtime → Supervisor) is threaded (2026-08-26 fix). The condense size guard prevents base-boundary on small graphs (base snapshot ≥ journal). The host surfaces `base-boundary` status faithfully when the engine returns it. The base-boundary status is reachable only on graphs large enough that the base snapshot is smaller than the pre-base journal. |
| **Journal adversarial gap: `scheduledDirtied` contents after undo** (GAP 2) | 2026-08-26 | **DEFERRED — lower value.** The return-shape test (J-return) asserts `scheduledDirtied` exists as a property. Asserting its exact contents couples the host test to engine internals. Defer until an agent-facing use case depends on knowing WHICH nodes were dirtied. |
| **Journal adversarial gap: `stackTopKind` / `redoTopKind` after ops** (GAP 3) | 2026-08-26 | **DEFERRED — lower value.** Same reasoning as GAP 2. The return-shape test asserts these fields exist; asserting their exact values couples the host test to engine internals. Defer until an agent uses these fields to decide whether to call undo/redo again. |
| **Journal adversarial gap: zod boundary rejects malformed input** (GAP 11) | 2026-08-26 | **DEFERRED — lower value.** The MCP server uses `z.enum(['undo','redo','replay'])` for the action field. The runtime's own guard (`unknown journal action`) is the second line of defense. The existing J-invalid + J-adversarial malformed tests cover the runtime boundary; the zod boundary is an SDK-level concern. |
| **Journal adversarial gap: renderer destruction mid-flight** (GAP 12) | 2026-08-26 | **DEFERRED — hard to test (async race).** The journal op's `await this.settleGate()` is vulnerable to renderer destruction mid-flight. The `RendererBackend`'s pending-entry cleanup rejects the pending promise. Defer to A2 hardening (per-request timeouts + reload re-arm). |

### E. ENVIRONMENT-BLOCKED → **⟶ ✅ DISCHARGED (2026-09-27, FOURTH pass)** — the LIVE-LEG re-run for `U-REALDOM-BOOT` (recorded 2026-09-27, third pass; **resolved 2026-09-27, fourth pass**)

**⟶ ✅ DISCHARGED (2026-09-27, FOURTH pass) — THE LIVE LEGS RAN AND ARE GREEN. This section is now a DATED RESOLVED RECORD, kept (not deleted) so no later pass re-opens a blocker that no longer exists.**

**⟶ AND ONE CLAUSE OF THIS SECTION'S GREEN EVIDENCE IS NOW CORRECTED (2026-09-27, SIXTH pass — the `F-1` RCA + fix; status/pointer only):** this section's discharge line quotes **`removed 2 scratch profile(s) … leftover profiles: NONE`** as *"the scratch cleanup **verified**"*. **That reading was WRONG as an END-STATE claim** — the report was **true at the moment it looked and false afterwards**: the leg's spawns went through `node_modules/.bin/electron` → `electron/cli.js`, a **wrapper whose child is the real app**, so the helper's kill left an **orphaned Electron main process** that **re-created the scratch profile AFTER the delete-and-verify sweep had reported success** (measured **4 of 4** repo-leg green runs; **31** roots accumulated in one session; the leftovers held **only Chromium artifacts and NO `provident-security.json`**, which is what made it invisible). **`F-1` is now CLOSED** — spawn the **BINARY** under a no-fallback-to-the-wrapper rule + register the **cleanup hook at `scratchRoot` creation** — and **the same report now matches the disk: ZERO roots on every exit path (green · malformed config · timeout-class exhaustion · `npm run ui`), checked immediately and after an 8 s settle, with 0 surviving `electron` processes.** See `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1 (the RCA, the two-part fix, the exit-path matrix and the **reporting-half-vs-END-STATE split**), `docs/decisions.md`'s two new ACTIVE rules and `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-LEFTOVER-PROFILES` (CLOSED host-side, **no `docs/HANDOFF.md` round owed**). **Nothing else in this section changes** — the legs did run green, and the discharge stands.

**THE ROOT CAUSE WAS NOT A HOST/SYSTEM CONDITION — IT WAS A CORRUPTED npm SHIM INSIDE THIS REPO'S `node_modules`**, and it is **REPAIRED**. **The single file:** **`node_modules/electron/cli.js`** — the file `node_modules/.bin/electron` **symlinks to**, and the entry point **every** Electron spawn here goes through — had been **corrupted into a self-executing loop** (`#!/bin/sh` … `cd …` … `exec …/node_modules/.bin/electron "$@"`), i.e. a **shell script that re-execs itself**, replacing the correct Node wrapper. **Every "boot" therefore re-entered `exec` forever at ~99 % CPU printing nothing — which is exactly the recorded symptom** (a **silent 99 %-CPU hang**, versus the *earlier*, **different**, fast-crash `SIGTRAP` mode). **The discriminating evidence:** `strace -f` showed an **unbroken cycle of `openat(… node_modules/.bin/electron)`** with the loader re-running each time and **no `clone`/`execve` progress**, `/proc/<pid>/status` read **`State: R (running)` at 99 % CPU**, and **0 `EPERM`/`EACCES` markers — no syscall denial at boot**. **The fix:** the **correct Node wrapper was restored** (copied from a **healthy install of the same package**, `electron` 44.4.3/44.4.5 — identical wrapper); **a boot probe then exits `0` in `176 ms` printing `BOOT-OK 44.4.5`**. **THE EVIDENCE THAT DISCHARGES THIS ITEM (all measured 2026-09-27, post-repair):** **`npm run divergence` → `R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`** (all **8** comparisons green, **census `12/12` both legs** — **⟶ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-27 (the `U-DIVERGENCE-EXT` (`C2`) DONE pass): the LIVE CENSUS IS `18/18` ON BOTH LEGS (`electron=18 shim=18`) because `U-GUTTER-UI` grew the demo envelope `12 → 18` nodes, so the `12/12` here is a DATED reading and must not be quoted as live** — `N = 9` intact) · **`npm run ui` → exit 0**, **`UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)`**, the **ONE** measurement **`427x22`** at **`fontSize="16px"`** via `provident.get_rendered_html`, **`retries=0`** (no retry needed), and the scratch cleanup **verified** — **`removed 2 scratch profile(s) … leftover profiles: NONE`**. **One environment noise line remains and is NOT a failure:** `dconf-CRITICAL … unable to create file '/run/user/1000/dconf/user': Permission denied` — **settings persistence only**. **WHAT THIS DISCHARGE DOES *NOT* CLAIM (stated so the resolved record is not over-read):** the retry's **runtime half** is **REACHABLE and drivable** (`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS` forces the timeout-class retry; `PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS=1` shows *"no retry"*), but **this pass did NOT induce a retry or an exhaustion** — the blind record's **`NBR-03`/`NBR-04`/`NBR-05` rows are NOT converted to passes**, and the unit stays **`LANDED-GREEN-BUT-NOT-DONE`** (its per-unit **documentation review** and the supervisor's **`DONE` row** remain). **The blocker row below is kept verbatim as the misattributed reading and is labelled WRONG — its two red herrings are named in the resolution note in `docs/next-steps.md`'s `WAVE-C UPDATE` block and in `docs/decisions.md`'s `ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER` row + `NPM-SHIM-INTEGRITY` lesson row.** **Nothing was written by this pass to `src/**`, `tests/**` or `scripts/**`** — the repaired path is an **install artifact**.

**⟶ QUEUED — THE PANE-DRAG COMPLIANCE UPDATE, WITH ITS CONTRACT (2026-10-03, THE ARCHITECT'S QUEUEING DIRECTIVE; `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE — the pending-rebuild mark above stands, and this block is the QUEUED work item's contract, to be implemented by a later spec-gated pass, NOT by this tracker row).** **THE ITEM: `QUEUED — the pane drag → zone update compliance update` (the §Q two-rule flip's first concrete rebuild), and its contract is the architect's own words, transcribed with the store vocabulary each clause maps onto:**

1. **CENTRAL STORAGE (the two-rule criterion, rule 1) — what the store now holds:** **pane/zone visibility, zone and pane sizes, and pane locations** are STORED values (the plan's §5.2.7 PROPOSED edges: `layout.zone.<id>.slot` · `layout.pane.<id>.size` · the zone's `minimized`/revealed state) — the drag flow reads and writes the STORE, never the host `this.layout` write-through (`setLayout` is superseded by store reads/writes for these values).
2. **PREVIEW IS TEMP-TIER DATA (rule 2 — listener-on-store):** **during the drag, the preview (the ghost/pane-in-flight) is committed as `temp`-tier data** — the store's per-episode tier — **and the commit of that temp value is what TRIGGERS the zone render**, via the store's event surface (the `set`/`commit` event arm; the zone render is a listener on the store, rule 2's shape). The preview is NOT a host-state presentation write; it is a store commit whose event fires the render.
3. **PROXIMITY DETECTION READS THE STORED SIZES:** **the drag's proximity detection on minimized and empty zones reads the zone sizes from the STORE** (the stored `layout.zone.<id>.size` / slot values), not from host geometry — the `threshold`/`withinProximity` comparison uses store-carried values (plan §5.2.7 row 4's caller-measured distance + the stored sizes).
4. **THE ZONE-SIZE CONSTRAINT, WITH ITS DISCARD AND ITS TWO-ARM REPAIR — RE-READ UNDER THE FUNCTION-BASED MODEL (2026-10-03, `D-GP-…-CONSTRAINTS-ARE-FUNCTIONS`; `RCA-8(d)` — the as-filed clause stands visible and this is its operative reading):** **zone size obeys a configured CONSTRAINT, and in the store's architecture a constraint is a PASSED FUNCTION, not a data row** (the ruling: *"a constraint is a passed function that the register/link calls with the changed data and the current and next states, and expects a boolean and optionally an error message / refusal reason in feedback from"*; **constraints are code features, not runtime data**). So the zone-size constraint is a **caller-supplied predicate function** — `(changedSize, current, next) => boolean` (optionally a reason) — supplied at construction as code, never added as runtime data. **THE DISCARD AND THE TWO-ARM REPAIR ARE THE CALLER-SUPPLIED REPAIR FUNCTION** (the ruling: *"a repair is a function called on a constraint violation, that takes corrective action to the data and outputs a success boolean and an optional error message"*): the repair function receives the violating size change and **arm (a)** ROUNDS a size in `[min/2, min)` UP to the configured minimum, and **arm (b)** DISCARDS a size below `min/2` — erasing the size change and MINIMIZING instead — returning its success boolean; the corrected contract pins the band boundary at exactly `min/2` with each sub-arm's outcome. The ruling notes the store machinery exists to CALL the passed functions on the register/link over the changed data + current + next states and to surface the repair's success/reason — the store authors no constraint ROW and no table schema, and `unique-path/tier` is a global graph behavioral state, NOT a constraint (the data-table `GraphConstraint` of the landed §2.7 is the superseded model, annotated at its sites by the re-derivation pass).
5. **RIGHT-CLICK = CANCEL = ERASE THE TEMP PREVIEW:** **a right-click during the drag ends the drag and ERASES the temp data made as preview** — a `remove`/`clear` of the `temp`-tier preview path — **letting the persistent original (the `file`-tier value) REASSERT** (a temp-only removal leaves the file value answering, the four-tier lifecycle's abandon path).
6. **RELEASE = COMMIT = TEMP TO FILE:** **releasing during the drag COMMITS the temp preview to the FILE tier, OVERWRITING the original settings** — the `commit('file.<path>', …)` regeneration: the temp preview is committed to `file`, overwriting the previous file value, with the lower-tier copies cleared per §2.8.

**THE TWO-OPEN-POINTS THE QUEUED CONTRACT CARRIES (recorded, NOT decided here — each is a spec-gate question for the implementing unit):** **(a) the constraint's REPAIR DOMAIN:** the store contract's `GraphConstraint.repair` is closed at `'next-surviving-by-order' | 'none'` (summary §2 / contract §2.7) — "translate to a minimize" is NOT in that closed domain, so the implementing unit owes either a contract amendment adding the repair outcome (with its own re-freeze consequence under the frozen-surface discipline) or an interpretation that the family-side minimum clamp (the landed `clampToBounds`, `gut.ts`) performs the translation and the constraint row's `refusalReason`/`repair:'none'` carries the discard — the unit's gate must name which; **and (b) the store edges' home:** the stored `layout.zone.*`/`layout.pane.*` values are the plan's §5.2.7 PROPOSED obligations gated by `U-STORE-MODULES`, so the implementing unit is admitted under that gate (possibly the plan's `U-STORE-MODULES` row, an architect admission), with the fork-side bytes rebuilt by the fork's own pass (H-r6). **OWNER: the foundation's `U-STORE-MODULES`-gated pass (or an architect-admitted successor) for the store edges + the fork's own pass for the fork's `sidebar-panes.ts`/`pane-drag.ts` consumption. QUEUE STATE: QUEUED — not admitted, no spec filed, no red set authored, no count moved (`21 DONE / 3 open` = `24`, `RCA-8(f)`); the queue row is this block, and its revisit condition is the implementing unit's admission.**
**⟶ THE OPEN-POINT (a) IS ANSWERED BY THE ARCHITECT'S RULING (2026-10-03, `D-GP-…-CONSTRAINTS-ARE-FUNCTIONS`; `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE — the open point above stands visible and this is its resolution, recorded so the implementing unit does not re-litigate it).** **The closed `repair`/`kind`/`refusalReason` DATA-TOKEN domain of the landed `§2.7` is the SUPERSEDED model: the ruling makes constraints and repairs PASSED FUNCTIONS ("code features, not data to be added during runtime"), removes the data-table schema entirely from the architecture (the store authors no constraint row and no table), and classes `unique-path/tier` as a global graph behavioral state rather than a constraint. The zone-size constraint's discard + two-arm repair is therefore the CALLER'S SUPPLIED REPAIR FUNCTION (arm (a) round-up at `[min/2, min)`; arm (b) discard-and-minimize below `min/2`), and the unit that implements the queued contract MUST re-derive the store's `GraphConstraint` machinery to the passed-function model (the landing pass's first duty: re-derive §2.7 + the `GraphConstraint` type + the frozen-surface artifacts' constraint rows, annotating the data-table form SUPERSEDED-BESIDE). The implementing unit's gate therefore does NOT choose between a contract amendment and a clamp interpretation — the ruling has made the choice: the passed-function model is the architecture, and the data-table rows of the landed artifacts are historical. THE OTHER OPEN POINT (b) (the store edges' `U-STORE-MODULES` gate) is UNCHANGED.**
**⟶ THE RE-DERIVATION IS DONE (2026-10-03, `CONSTRAINTS-ARE-PASSED-FUNCTIONS` LANDED — ANNOTATE-BESIDE, the resolution above was its own dated reading; this block is the landing's record): the contract's §2.7 + the `GraphConstraint` type + the store artifact's field 2 + the register-row `constraintId` cell were RE-DERIVED to the function-carrier; the artifacts RE-FROZEN (`AMENDMENT CONSTRAINT-RE-DERIVE-1`, new store digest `sha256:2933fcb8…` over `:40`–`:432`, input unchanged `dfee6ee0…`); the module wave's gate 3/4 RE-RUN (red re-authored CR1–CR7, 5 machinery rows red → 87/87 green → GATE 4 EMPTY); and the consuming module `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` conformed within the licence (integration 33/33 re-verified, trio clean, legacy 11 → 11 name-identical, no other suite regressed).** **THE QUEUED PANE-DRAG CONTRACT IS NOW EXPRESSIBLE ON THE CONFORMED MACHINERY:** the zone-size constraint is the caller-supplied constraint function (size-band check over changed/current/next), and the two-arm repair is the caller-supplied repair function (arm (a) round-up at `[min/2, min)`; arm (b) discard-and-minimize below `min/2`) — landing corrective action in the same committed write, observable via `repaired[]` + the `cause:'repair'` event, with refusal-via-feedback as the repairless fallback. **THE QUEUE ITEM STAYS QUEUED** (not admitted; its remaining gate is open point (b), the store edges' `U-STORE-MODULES` home); this annotation records only that the rule-change prerequisite is met. NO COUNT MOVES (`21 DONE / 3 open` = `24`; `RCA-8(f)`).** **⟶ MEASURED CORRECTION 2026-10-03 (THE `U-STORE-DOC-SWEEP` PASS, `H4`, THE SUPERVISOR'S OWN RUN — `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE: the as-filed `11` figure above is KEPT VISIBLE and this clause is the measured reading).** **`npx vitest run tests/store-core-graph.test.ts` MEASURES `12 failed | 59 passed (71)` — NOT `11`.** The twelve failing row names, read from the run: `§4.1(red) · §3.5 R-11` · `§3.4 R-12` (the `R-12(c)` positive-control reading) · `§5.1 · the DENIED set` · `M-2` · `F-6` · `F-18` · `M-10` · `M-11` · `F-9` · `M-7` · `REGISTER-EXEC` · `REGISTER-UNRUN` — all T9-class residue (the superseded data-table constraint fixture + the pre-conformance readings + the absent-module static rows), byte-unchanged and unedited. **The `11` was accurate at the conformance pass's own tree and is stale at this head; a pass that quotes it must quote `12`.**

**⟶ `E10` (`U-GUTTER-UI`) SPEC FILED — `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` (2026-09-27, the spec pass after the admission ruling).** The contract's cells: the affordance is **provident-rendered data** in this repo's own demo envelope (`src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` = the authoring site; one new authored gutter card), driving a NEW `src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts` (**`4 + 7 = 11` names**; four values — `createGutterAffordance`, `cursorDeclarationFor`, `domEventSource`, `sizeFromPointer` — and exactly THREE imports: `clampToBounds` from `./gutter.js`, plus two type-only) which **COMPOSES `E3`'s controller**. It owns the coordinate source (the only place a pointer is read), the cursor mapping (caller-supplied `cursorOf`, **no cursor literal in the module**), the transient preview channel (**never the sink**), the capture decision (its own install path), the drop-revert and every rendered-geometry claim. **THE FOUR PARKED `E3` OBLIGATIONS ARE ITS FIRST ROWS** (the throwing-hook discard with ZERO session calls after a terminal; the closed session read set with the invented seam GONE; the disposed-session short-circuit; the two-writer divergence on the real composition). The register is **7 rows / `142` declared** printed with its terms (`22+10+15+45+20+18+12`; SM 47 · IM 65 · TP 30) with 2 `(bounded)` rows and three declared-vs-distinct pairs (`45/15`, `18/6`, `12/10`); the honesty block says plainly that **every rendered fact belongs to the live leg**. The `§5.U` delta-matrix is **7 U-rows** (`U-1`…`U-7`) with the coverage-report schema and the read-only audit clauses, and **`U-GAP-1`** records that `docs/specs/user-flow-audit.md` still does not exist (sixth confirmation). **THE LIVE BATTERY IS MANDATORY** (`npm run ui` + the app driven through `npm run mcp -- --target http` — the repo ships no `scripts/live-drive.mjs`, and the MCP surface carries no coordinates, so the pointer half is exercised by the real-DOM leg and by synthetic event objects through the source seam), parked only for a STRUCTURAL reason. **THREE OPEN QUESTIONS, each with a working default and a stated block, awaiting the architect (`§7a.1`):** (1) the hover-time axis/cursor evaluation budget (default: the module's own `axisOf` on the hover path with ZERO controller calls); (2) the invalid-release timing (default: take the invalid arm from the drag while the gesture is STILL ACTIVE, because `E3`'s `reset` is legal only for an active gesture and the session's own `pointerup` ends it); (3) the preview channel's write form (default: a transient presentation write through the caller's `applyPreview`, with the committed state in the graph — a mid-drag graph dispatch would replace the element the session's listener sits on). **NOTE FOR THE NEXT PASS: `E10` reached its spec WITHOUT a gate-1 proposal review (the architect's instruction went straight to the spec). The four-step review should therefore run ON THE FILED CONTRACT (validity · critique · architecture · change-analysis) before any red set — reviewing the actual contract is cheaper and stronger than reviewing the proposal was.**

### I-octies. `E10` (`U-GUTTER-UI`) GATE 1, STEPS 1–2 RAN ON THE FILED CONTRACT — steps 3–4 DEFERRED, the spec needs a repair pass (2026-09-27)

**⟶ ANNOTATION BESIDE THE ROW ABOVE (2026-10-01, THE DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL AMENDMENT PASS; `RCA-8(d)`: THE ROW ABOVE IS KEPT BYTE-FOR-BYTE AND NO CITED CLAUSE IS REWRITTEN).** **THE `live-drive` CITATION IN THAT ROW READS *"the repo ships no `scripts/live-drive.mjs`"*, AND THAT HALF IS STILL TRUE — the file does not exist in this tree (`scripts/` holds exactly `electron-divergence.mjs`, `electron-spawn.mjs`, `electron-ui.mjs`, `mcp-cli.mjs`, measured by this pass's own read of the directory).** **WHAT THE ANNOTATION ADDS IS THE MISSING POSITIVE HALF — WHICH DRIVER OWNS WHICH LEG, since a later pass must not go looking for `live-drive.mjs` and must not name it as this repo's:** **the `ui` leg is driven by `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`** · **the MCP route is driven by `scripts/mcp-cli.mjs`** (`npm run mcp -- --target battery|http …`) · **the divergence leg is driven by `scripts/electron-divergence.mjs`** (with `scripts/electron-spawn.mjs` as its precondition) — **and the `live-drive.mjs` that the gate-supervisor instructions' mandatory-live-battery clause names *"e.g."* is the FORK's driver (`../Astrographer/`), NOT this repo's.** **The finding itself is recorded at the plan's `§9` `Q-11` and its `§8.7` item (5); the plan's `§10`'s provenance note that the same finding *"is a spec's measurement quoted, not my own glob"* is superseded by this pass's own directory read.** **NO STATUS WORD IN THE ROW ABOVE MOVES: the battery remains MANDATORY, and the pointer half remains exercised by the real-DOM leg and by synthetic event objects through the source seam, exactly as that row says.**

**VERDICT: `VALID-WITH-CONDITIONS` (validity) + `FLAWED` (critique). `U-GUTTER-UI` IS NOT DELEGABLE AS FILED.** The four-step review was run **on the filed contract** (cheaper and stronger than reviewing the proposal, as `docs/pending.md` §I-septies recorded); steps 3–4 (architecture · change-analysis) are **DEFERRED until the contract is repaired**, because reviewing a moving target would waste them.

**WHAT THE REVIEW CAUGHT — and it is the E3 lesson applied for free (one pass instead of three repair rounds):**
- **[DECISIVE] NO COMPOSITION EXISTS IN THE APP.** `§5.1`'s allow-list admits only `src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts` + one authored card in `demo-envelope.ts`; `§2.2`'s prohibitions forbid any other import and all element lookup; and `§5.2` itself states the renderer bundle does not import the module. So **hover, press, drag, release and drop never occur in any running app**: the unit chartered as *"the place the family becomes observable"* is **observability-empty by construction**, and `§5.U`'s `U-3`–`U-6` ("MEASURED at the live gate") are unreachable. The critique's strongest counter-case (decline `E10` outright, or re-scope it to an **in-app wiring** unit) is **not answerable by clause work inside the current diff scope** — it needs an architect ruling (below).
- **THE DRAG HALF HAS NO READING SOURCE.** `§2.3`'s transition table attaches exactly three listeners (`pointerover`/`pointerout`/`pointerdown`) and `M-4` asserts "exactly THREE `source.on` calls"; the module is forbidden to replicate the session's `'pointermove'` constant; and the session's `onMove` receives **no event**. So *"on drag: show the resized state from the current cursor position"* — the charter's central clause — is **unimplementable as written**, and `U-4` has nothing to drive.
- **THE CAPTURE DECISION HAS NO LANDING SITE.** The spec requires `capture: true` in the object handed to `controller.attach(element, hooks)`, but `E3`'s `attach(element, hooks?)` builds exactly the four hooks and forwards no `capture`; `E3`'s sealed clause says the opposite twice. Either `E3`'s denied path gains the argument (**a new gate on a `DONE`-adjacent module**) or the capture claim is withdrawn and `F-11`/`I-4` become vacuous.
- **THE REGISTER IS A MIS-SUM — the SIXTH in this family.** `§5.5.1`'s cells print `22, 15, 15, 45, 20, 18, 12 = 147` while `§5.5.3`/`§5.3` print `22, 10, 15, 45, 20, 18, 12 = 142` (`P-GU-SM-2` is `10` in one place and `15` in another), and `docs/pending.md` §I-septies quotes the wrong figure too. `P-GU-SM-1`'s `12` and `P-GU-TP-1`'s `18` also **re-inherit `E3`'s audited over-strength class** (assertions counted as drives — the `IM-4` defect).
- **THE `[U]` ROWS AND THE COVERAGE REPORT HAVE NO INSTRUMENT.** `§5.2`'s `[U]` rows (i)–(iv) are not observable by the declared leg (`ci-ui-leg.md` fixes the leg at ONE measurement and five rows `R0`–`R4`, and states it is not an attribute/geometry proof); no allowed tool emits `§5.U`'s JSON coverage report (`electron-ui.mjs` prints only `UI RESULT: …`; `mcp-cli.mjs` has no such schema); and the pointer half cannot come from `provident.dispatch` (no coordinates).
- **SMALLER MUST-FIXES:** the import census (`createGestureSession` must be called yet a value import of the session is forbidden — what does `E3`'s `session` seam receive?); the **value channel** (`sizeFor` is absent from the five seams the spec supplies, so a valid release cannot commit) — `PointerResolver` declared but used nowhere (`4+7` vs `4+6`); the cursor target (the hovered affordance vs the target element); the invalid arm leaving the preview at its last valid value; the two-writer falsifier depending on the very defect it exists to expose; `M-1`–`M-3` being green-able only by the DENIED `E3` path; and four dangling/misrouted citations (`M-8b`, `P-7`→`R-11`, `P-9`→`F-13`/`I-10`, `R-9`'s scope).

**TWO RULINGS THE ARCHITECT OWES BEFORE THE SPEC CAN BE REPAIRED:**
1. **WHO COMPOSES THE AFFORDANCE IN THE RUNNING APP, AND FROM WHICH ALLOWED PATH?** The charter requires observability; the current diff scope forbids the only place it could live (`src/renderer/**`, and the demo envelope's wiring). Options: (a) admit a bounded renderer/demo wiring edit to this unit (a diff-scope change with its own gate); (b) re-scope `E10` to an **in-app wiring** unit and defer the affordance's own spec; (c) accept a `[T]`-only unit and rename its charter honestly — in which case the architect's own reason for parking `E3` applies to it too.
2. **THE INVALID-RELEASE TIMING.** The filed default takes the invalid arm from the **drag** while the gesture is still active — but `E3`'s `reset` is a TERMINAL (it commits once and ends the gesture), so *"on release: … otherwise, reset"* would be discharged **mid-drag**, the `end` terminal could never follow, and a re-press would be required. Either accept a reset at the first invalid observed move (and lose the release semantics as written) or route the timing question to the session (**a `U-GSESSION` gate**).

**Owner: the architect (the two rulings) + a SpecWriter repair pass (the must-fixes and the citation repairs) + the deferred steps 3–4 (architecture · change-analysis) — then, and only then, the red set. Meanwhile `E3`'s checks stay PARKED (§I-septies) and nothing else in the repo is blocked. Layer note: this review cost ONE pass and found the observability hole that `E3`'s cycle discovered only after three repair rounds — the gate order, run on a filed contract, is what to keep.**

### I-nonies. `E10` (`U-GUTTER-UI`) SPEC REPAIRED under the architect's ruling; the renderer wiring is ADMITTED (2026-09-27)

**THE RULING:** *"Yes the UI change is allowed to touch the renderer — the UI needs to be rendered."* An `ACTIVE` row now records it permanently (`docs/decisions.md`: `UI-UNITS-MAY-TOUCH-THE-RENDERER-WIRING (a diff scope is a DERIVATION, never a template)`), together with the rule it pins — **each unit's DENIED set is derived from that unit's own charter, never copied from a sibling, and gate 1 must answer *"does the allowed file set contain a path from the application's entry point to this code?"* for any unit whose charter claims observability.** The defect: `E10`'s first filing denied `src/renderer/**` **by inheritance** from the sibling `src/shared` mechanism units, which deleted the only site where the affordance could be instantiated and left the unit **observability-empty**.

**WHAT THE REPAIR LANDED in `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` (all in place, as-filed text kept visible, nothing renumbered):**
- **R1 — the scope.** `src/renderer/renderer.ts` + one bounded helper in `src/renderer/runtime.ts` enter the ALLOW-LIST (rows `10`/`11`) for exactly **four wiring roles + one graph read**; `src/main/**`, the preload/MCP surface, the shell chrome, the configs, `scripts/**`, every sibling `src/shared/*` module and **both FROZEN pairs** stay DENIED; a renderer edit that hand-writes DOM is a finding. **WHO/WHERE/HOW ARE NOW ANSWERED: `startGutterAffordance(runtime)` in `src/renderer/renderer.ts` constructs the session and the `E3` controller, resolves the affordance/target from the producing graph via `Runtime.elementForNodeId(...)`, calls `createGutterAffordance(...)` and attaches; the state is reached by `npm start` and read by `npm run mcp -- --target http --port 3787 targets|html|dispatch`.** The three self-contradicting clauses that produced the hole (the leg-3 "the demo does NOT import it" note, the `R-8x` "imported by the demo envelope only" branch, `§1` item 6's "no renderer file changes") are deleted with dated tombstones.
- **R2 — the live observables.** Every `U`-row states its exact command and observed value; `npm run ui` is corrected (it measures **none** of these rows directly — it takes ONE measurement on its own probe envelope and contributes its mandatory `[U]` green); `U-3`/`U-4`/`U-6` are labelled **`NOT-OBSERVABLE-BY-ANY-SHIPPED-INSTRUMENT`** (a `MANUAL OPERATOR` record under a real pointer); the `§5.U` coverage report is ruled a **MANUAL record** with its quoted field list and `"emitter": "MANUAL"` (no shipped instrument emits it).
- **R3 — the register (the family's SIXTH mis-sum, corrected).** `P-GU-SM-2` is ruled **`15`** from its own drive table; the DECLARED total is **`140` = `22+15+15+45+20+18+12`** (chain `22→37→52→97→117→135→140`; subtotals `SM 52 · IM 65 · TP 30`); `P-GU-SM-1`'s `12` and `P-GU-TP-1`'s `18` are **re-granulated with their honest distinct-drive figures printed beside the declared terms** (`22/15`, `18/12` — the `E3` PBT audit's over-strength class, not repeated silently).
- **R4 — the pointer channel.** The module attaches **its own `'pointermove'` listener** through the source seam using a caller-supplied token (`moveTypeOf`, degrading to the non-pointer `'move'`) that never replicates the session's private constant; the module's own `'pointerdown'` turn is the secondary-press observation point; per-set counts are stated (**four** module vs **one** session) with the ordering and the before-capture case.
- **R5 — capture WITHDRAWN.** `E3`'s frozen `attach` cannot carry the opt-in, so the claim is withdrawn from all three clause sites, `F-11`/`I-4` are **deleted with tombstones** (`F-14`/`I-13` replace them), and the need is a named **`E3`-SIDE owed item** with its revisit condition and the honest UX consequence (the live reading stops when the pointer leaves the element).
- **R6 — the value channel.** The affordance's own `onMove` calls **`handle.set(clampedValue)`** (the session's documented consumer-side channel) and `E3`'s terminal commits the clamped value read back through `sizeFor: (element, gesture) => gesture.value` — no new seam invented, and a valid drag can commit.
- **R7 — the invalid-release resolution.** A VALID release is the session's own `pointerup` ⇒ `end` ⇒ exactly one commit of the clamped dragged value; an INVALID drag takes the **`reset` terminal the moment a SEAM FAILURE is established** (unusable pair/default, discarded record) ⇒ exactly one commit of the clamped pre-drag size, the handle cleared and the visible state reverted — an ordinary out-of-bounds value is merely CLAMPED and stays valid. The divergence from "decide at the release instant" is stated, with the `U-GSESSION` alternative named as rejected-for-now.
- **R8 — the rest.** The import census is stated (`clampToBounds` + two type-only; the session arrives as a wiring-supplied argument; `4 + 7 = 11`); `PointerResolver` is wired as `pointerOf`; the cursor target is the **hovered affordance** at every call site; the invalid arm **reverts**; the two-writer falsifier is replaced by a **live-composition two-reading check** (no longer depending on the invented seam); **`M-1`/`M-2`/`M-3` are marked `EXPECTED-RED` against the landed `E3` with their `E3`-side owner and a DONE-row print shape that forbids "green"**; and the four citation defects are repaired.

**STILL OWED (named so a fresh supervisor does not inherit a stale picture):** (a) **the deferred gate-1 steps 3–4** (architecture · change-analysis) on this repaired text, before any red set; (b) the **`§7a.1` items 1 and 3** defaults remain unruled (hover evaluation budget; the preview write form) — recommendations as filed; (c) the **`E3`-SIDE capture opt-in** and the three **`E3`-SIDE host defects** (`docs/pending.md` §I-sexies) remain parked with the rest of `E3`'s checks; (d) if the gate prefers the **distinct-drive figures AS the declared terms**, the total becomes `140 → 127` and three sites move together; (e) two role lists (`§0A` note 9, `§2.6` item 1) still describe the module as owning the capture opt-in and should be swept in the next pass; (f) the new `⟶ RECORDED` block sits after the file-end note as a deliberate dated exception.

### I-decies. `E10` GATE 1 STEP 3 (architecture) RAN on the repaired contract — `DELEGABLE-AFTER-SPEC`, with TEN conditions and THREE escalations (2026-09-27)

**VERDICT: `DELEGABLE-AFTER-SPEC`.** The seam, the authorship boundary and the channels are close to right, but **four interface facts remain unpinned** (each a place where a TestWriter and an Implementer will diverge) and one clause pair is internally contradictory. Step 4 (change-analysis) is deferred until the conditions below land, so it reviews a stable text.

**THE GOVERNING FINDING (fixable inside the existing allow-list, no new file, no new export): `EventSourceLike.on/off` is declared with a ZERO-ARGUMENT handler, so the module's own listeners are COORDINATE-BLIND — the live pointer can reach `resolveEventPointer`/`pointerOf` ONLY if the WIRING's source forwards the DOM event to the handler as its first argument, and the spec never says so.** This — not an instrument limit — is why the pointer-driven `U`-rows had to be demoted to `MANUAL OPERATOR`: **the live path is not wired at all.** The fix is a wiring role (event forwarding) plus a clause (the module's handler takes `(event: unknown)`), and both are inside the current scope.

**CONDITIONS THE SPEC MUST LAND (C-1…C-10):** **C-1** pin `elementForNodeId(id)`'s **id space** (engine `nodeId` → authored `css.id` → `props.id`, the precedence `resolveTarget` already uses), its **attribute-walk-only** resolution from the mount (no `querySelector`/`closest`), its totality, its **exact call site** (`main()`, immediately after `runtime.bootstrap()`, before the bridge conditional, nowhere else) and the **null discipline** (unresolvable ⇒ `attach()` false ⇒ a no-op, never a throw) — note `Runtime` has **no** equivalent method today, so it is genuinely new; **C-2** declare the source handlers as **`(event: unknown) => void`** and state that the wiring's source is the ONLY DOM-event observer and forwards it (both the move turn and the pointerdown turn must use it); **C-3** rule the module's move type as **`POINTER_TYPES.move`, obtained by the wiring** (the session exports the constant — the declared opaque-token fallback `'move'` is nobody's DOM event and converts a miswiring into a **silent no-op that still goes green**: the false-green class `B-9`/`P-9` exist to close; if the opaque seam is kept, rule the degradation and add a row asserting the **type matched the session's**); **C-4** state the listener **ownership** (the module's four vs the session's install/tracking set, both on the same element, removed by their own owner, the wiring removing nothing); **C-5** name the **commit sink's write route** (one managed-channel write on the runtime — `Runtime.applyCommand`'s `state-slice` shape — NOT a preview write and NOT the authored handler body), bind `§2.5` item 5's re-render hazard to the **commit** as well as the preview, and state whether that write preserves the affordance element's identity or needs a rebind; **C-6** restate **`M-2` to the module-observable form** — as filed it is **UNSATISFIABLE from this unit** (`M-1` forbids the module from referencing `disposed` while `M-2` requires it to honour it; the `E3`-side short-circuit is the only remedy, and `M-1`'s runtime half is NOT expected-red); **C-7** correct `P-GU-SM-1`'s declared-term derivation (its `22` is derived as `10 drives + 12 ASSERTIONS` — the `E3` `IM-4` class; the term must not be derived from assertions) and mark `P-GU-TP-1` (and `P-GU-SM-1`, or justify not) `(bounded)`; **C-8** bind a `MANUAL` row to a real open window and a recorded command sequence (the `npm start` invocation + the observation + the accompanying `targets`/`html` readings with values) — an operator's memory is not evidence; **C-9** give the **eleven demo seams a home** (see E-1) and sweep the three stale clauses the repair left (`§2.6` item 3, `§7` item 13's capture claim, `§2.1` item 7's "no renderer file changes"); **C-10** state, per live row, which party is the instrument (module drive / wiring live path / MCP tools / operator).

**ESCALATIONS:** **E-1 (blocks the red set)** — the eleven caller seams (`sizeFromPointer`, `axisOf`, `cursorOf`, `applyPreview`, `applyCursor`, `startSizeOf`, `boundsOf`, `resizableOf`, `commit`, `pointerOf`, `moveTypeOf`) have **no permitted file**: `demo-envelope.ts` currently exports data only, and `renderer.ts` must not hold demo policy. **Recommendation: extend `§5.1` row 2 to let `src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` additionally export the demo's SEAM BUNDLE** (imported by `renderer.ts` only) — one allow-list cell, and it keeps the axis/cursor/size policy out of the renderer. **E-2 (blocks `M-5`/`M-8` and the second live gesture)** — rule the sink's write route and the **element-reuse question**: if the terminal's graph write replaces the affordance element, the affordance is single-shot in the app and the wiring owes a rebind (a fifth role) — an architect-visible choice. **E-3 (minor)** — if the gate prefers the honest distinct figures AS the declared terms, the total moves `140 → 127` and **three sites move together**.

**RESIDUAL RISKS recorded:** the "same built tree" claim for `npm run ui`/`npm run divergence` is not mechanically true (both scripts rebuild); leg 3's "five bundles" wording is really four esbuild outputs + a copied `index.html`; a re-derivation after boot (`load`/`code.load`) tears the graph down and the wiring does not re-attach (state it as out-of-contract or a rebind obligation); the module-vs-session listener-ordering guarantee should be asserted **from the source-call log**, not from a real-DOM premise; and `M-1`'s static half stays red-by-design so the DONE row must print its **measured** call counts.

**NEXT: a SpecWriter pass landing C-1…C-10 (with the E-1 allow-list cell and the E-2 ruling), then gate-1 step 4 (change-analysis) on the stable text, then the red set. `E3`'s defect resolution (`docs/pending.md` §I-sexies `E3`-HOST-1/2/3 + §I-octies) is QUEUED BEHIND IT per the architect's ordering, and remains small: three bounded module edits plus their regression rows.**

### I-undecies. THE CALLER SEAMS ARE THE FAMILY'S DOWNSTREAM CONTRACT — the architect's ruling, and what it changes (2026-09-27)

**THE RULING (verbatim): *"The caller seams will need to be used by the downstream projects, these are not just demo tools."*** An `ACTIVE` row now records it (`docs/decisions.md`: `GUTTER-CALLER-SEAMS-ARE-THE-FAMILY'S-DOWNSTREAM-CONTRACT`).

**WHAT IT CHANGES — the family's seam set (`sizeFromPointer` · `axisOf` · `cursorOf` · `applyPreview` · `applyCursor` · `startSizeOf` · `boundsOf` · `resizableOf` · `commit` · `pointerOf` · `moveTypeOf`) is a PUBLIC EXPORTED CONTRACT for downstream implementers, not demo internals:**
1. **The seam TYPES are part of the affordance module's exported census** (`src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts`), so a fork can import them; each seam's **signature, REQUIRED/OPTIONAL status, totality rule and DECLARED DEGRADATION** is normative contract text.
2. **Every seam's degradation must be observable:** absent / non-callable / throwing ⇒ a **declared safe default** with a row that can FAIL — **never a silent no-op** (this is exactly the class the `E3` false-green findings named; a fork's miswiring must be visible, not swallowed).
3. **This repo's demo supplies ONE implementation** (a consumer/sample): its home is a **demo-side file** and it must be **named as an implementation, never as the contract**. The contract is the module's exported types plus the spec's seam table.
4. **`E-1` (gate-1 escalation, `docs/pending.md` §I-decies) is ANSWERED by this ruling** — its proposal ("let `demo-envelope.ts` export the demo's seam bundle as the deliverable") is **REJECTED as the contract**; what `§5.1` may additionally allow is a bounded home for the demo's **example implementation** only, and `renderer.ts` must import the **module's** seam types (not a demo-owned contract shape).
5. **`docs/FORKER.md` must carry the seam contract** — names, signatures, required/optional, degradation, and what a fork must supply — with the same care the `SCH-*` dispositions gave to the delegate surfaces; the fork's own `SCH-6` refile (`"no capture before establishment"`, axis/threshold/commit for zone sizes) is answered *through* these seams, so this is the downstream-facing API of the family.
6. **`E10`'s register and rows must pin the per-seam degradation** (one row per seam class, or a table row plus the drives), and the spec's `§2.1`/`§2.4`/`§2.6` seam tables become **contract**, not example.

### I-duodecies. `E-2` CLOSED ON THE ARCHITECT'S ANSWER: **THE RUNTIME REUSES THE EMITTED ELEMENT** (2026-09-27)

**THE RULING (architect, 2026-09-27): *"Reuse"*** — the runtime **REUSES** the emitted element across a graph write (a `state-slice` apply patches the existing element in place; **element identity is preserved**). **E-2's element-reuse question is therefore ANSWERED, and its option 2 (a fifth wiring role with a rebind) is REJECTED — no rebind role is needed.**

**WHAT FOLLOWS, and what the spec must now state:**
1. **No fifth wiring role.** `§5.1`'s wiring roles stay at **four** (+ the one graph-read helper); the diff scope does **not** grow. The affordance's element identity is stable across the commit, so the module's four listeners and the session's `install` survive a terminal; the affordance is **NOT single-shot**, and a repeat `attach` remains the pinned first-config-wins no-op (nothing needs re-installing).
2. **A clause is owed: the commit's write must not STRUCTURALLY replace the affordance node.** Reuse covers a *patch*; it does not cover a write that adds, removes or moves the affordance's own node (or its authored card's structure), which would still change the element. **The sink's target is the authored STATUS/readout node, deliberately outside the affordance's node** — that is the authored-layout consequence of the ruling, and it is a **downstream-facing rule** for forks (it belongs beside the seam table in `docs/FORKER.md`: *"write your committed value to a node outside the affordance's own node; the affordance element is reused, and a structural write through its node is yours to avoid"*).
3. **The evidence owed is one LIVE observation, cheap and decisive:** a **second gesture on the same affordance after a commit** (drag → commit → drag again) in the running app, recording that the affordance still resolves to the **same element object** (same `data-node-id` **and** object identity across the write) and that the second drag commits. This is the row that would have caught the single-shot failure the node suite is structurally blind to; it is filed under the mandatory live battery, not as a `[T]` row.
4. **`E-2`'s OTHER half is still open and unchanged:** the **sink's write ROUTE** must still be named in the spec (`C-5`: one managed-channel write on the runtime via `Runtime.applyCommand`'s `state-slice` shape — **not** a preview write and **not** the authored handler body), and the re-render hazard clause (`§2.5` item 5) is now bound to the **commit** as well as to the preview, with the reuse ruling as the reason the commit is safe for element identity (but not for a structural write).
5. **E-3 remains the only other open architect choice** on this unit (whether the honest distinct-drive figures become the declared register terms: `140 → 127`, three sites moving together).

### I-terdecies. `E-3` DECIDED: **RE-GRAIN** — the declared register terms become DRIVE counts (2026-09-27)

**THE RULING (architect, 2026-09-27): *"Re-grain."*** An `ACTIVE` row now records the convention permanently (`docs/decisions.md`: **`A DECLARED REGISTER TERM IS A DRIVE COUNT (assertions and readings are printed BESIDE it, never counted in it)`**).

**WHAT IT CHANGES FOR `E10` — three sites move TOGETHER, no site may print a total that is not the sum of its own printed terms:**
- `P-GU-SM-1`: **`22` → `15`** (its 12 mid-drag items are ASSERTIONS over 5 drives, not drives).
- `P-GU-TP-1`: **`18` → `12`** (its `stats()`/`controller` items are READINGS of the same state, not drives).
- **DECLARED TOTAL: `134` = `15 + 15 + 15 + 45 + 20 + 12 + 12`** (chain `15 → 30 → 45 → 90 → 110 → 122 → 134`; subtotals **`SM 45` · `IM 65` · `TP 24`** = `134`). Caps: largest row `45 ≤ 100`, total `134 ≤ 400`, stop-after-5 untriggered.
- **THE MIS-SUM IS RESOLVED BY THE RE-GRAIN, not by an annotation:** the file currently prints the total as **`140`** while its own printed terms sum to **`147`** — the seventh instance of the class in this family. After the re-grain the printed terms *are* `15, 15, 15, 45, 20, 12, 12` and the printed total **is** their sum (`134`).
- The **assertion and reading ledgers stay** and are printed **beside** the declared terms (`22` → the honest `15` drives + `12` assertions recorded as a separate figure; `18` → `12` drives + `6` readings likewise) — they are evidence of coverage, never part of a term.

**WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT UNIT (recorded so a later pass does not re-open it):** the harnesses count `attemptsRun` as **drives**, so a non-drive declared term can never reconcile with the measured figure — that is exactly how `E3` came to print `P-GT-IM-1` `18`/`20` and `P-GT-IM-4` `28`/`27`, and how `IM-4`'s "`18` = three drives copied six times" over-strength survived a PBT audit. **One convention now closes the class: a declared term is a drive count.** The caps buy nothing from an inflated term, and `E3`'s parked residues become retro-closable by the same rule when its checks resume.

**QUEUED (this pass could not land them: the `C-1…C-10` + seam-contract repair is still IN FLIGHT on `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md`, and a second writer on that file would be a `RCA-8` hazard) — the NEXT amendment pass must land, in ONE commit:**
1. **the `E-3` re-grain** at the three sites (`§5.5.1`'s two cells + their assertion/reading ledgers, `§5.5.3`'s arithmetic + chain + subtotals, `§5.3` item 11's DONE-row requirement) so the printed total **is** the sum of the printed terms (`134`);
2. **`E-2`'s three items** (`docs/pending.md` §I-duodecies): the clause that the commit's write must **not structurally replace the affordance node** (the sink targets the authored status/readout node, deliberately outside it); the **downstream rule** for `docs/FORKER.md` beside the seam table; and the **live second-gesture observation** that closes E-2's evidence half (same element object across a commit + a committed second drag) — replacing the "outstanding architect choice" marking with the closed ruling;
3. **`E-2`'s other half** — the sink's write **ROUTE** (`C-5`) if the in-flight repair did not already pin it;
4. whatever the in-flight repair reports as unfinished (its own list), and the `§7a.1` items still open.

## §K. THE `threshold` SEMANTICS RCA AND ITS REQUESTED HARNESS MODIFICATIONS (2026-09-27 — **REQUESTS, NOT LANDED RULINGS**; the architect's to adopt, amend or decline)

**THE RCA IS `docs/specs/rca-cross-project-handoff-semantics.md`** (this pass). In one line: the upstream fork's `SCH-7` ask was adopted into `U-RELOCATE` with **a parameter name and no meaning** (`threshold`), the receiving repo's own prohibition tables had independently classified **the same identifier as banned vocabulary**, and no gate step asks a reader to notice either fact — so the unit reached gate 1 with a requirement that **could not be read**, and the ambiguity was deferred twice (spec → architect) before the architect supplied the whole behaviour (**zone expands to visibility inside the proximity + a ghost preview + release-outside ⇒ the reset arm; the threshold IS that distance** — now recorded as `docs/decisions.md`'s ACTIVE row `U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE`).

| # | Request | Owner | Acceptance test / what it blocks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **K-1** | **A HANDOFF-DRAFT SPECIALIST ROLE** (`role_handoff_drafter`) that runs **between an external ask's adoption and the unit's spec filing**, producing an **ADOPTION DOSSIER**: (1) a **SEMANTICS TABLE** — one row per adopted identifier (`what it measures/decides · unit · domain · supplier · evaluator · where the arithmetic lives · the observable that proves it · the clause that pins it · STATUS`), with any `undefined-until-answered` row **blocking** delegability; (2) a **COLLISION REPORT** (every adopted identifier grepped against this repo's `P-*`/`R-*`/`S-11` prohibition tables, with a recorded reconciliation for each hit); (3) a **LAYER MAP** (per behaviour: `[T]`/`[H]`/`[U]`/`[D]`, or the three-part refusal when nothing here can prove it); (4) the **DEFINITION-OF-TERMS block** that goes INTO the unit's spec; (5) the **OPEN-SEMANTICS LIST** with the architect questions; (6) the **FORK-FACING RESTATEMENT** for `docs/FORKER.md` | the architect (adoption) then the harness | `E4` replayed against it yields the missing definitions **before** a spec is filed; `threshold` cannot reach gate 1 undefined again |
| **K-2** | **`H-1` — A PARAMETER-SEMANTICS GATE**: gate 1's change-analysis may not return `DELEGABLE` for an externally-adopted unit with any `undefined-until-answered` row; the verdict vocabulary gains **`BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS`** with the open list attached | `AGENTS.md` items 8/9 + the gate-1 record shape | this session's `FLAWED` becomes one word at step 4 |
| **K-3** | **`H-2` — THE CROSS-BOUNDARY COLLISION CHECK** as a gate step (a token may be banned in one layer and legitimate in another; the adoption must say which and why) | the dossier + the gate-1 checklist | `grep -rn "\bthreshold\b" docs/` returns both families; the dossier must name both |
| **K-4** | **`H-3` — NO BARE IDENTIFIER IN A CONTRACT**: a spec may not declare a parameter without a semantics row (unit/domain/referent/evaluator); a missing row is a spec-gate finding, as a missing return shape is | `AGENTS.md` item 5 + the spec format | `docs/specs/relocate.md` cannot be filed with `threshold` undefined |
| **K-5** | **`H-4` — STOP QUOTING SIGNATURES AS ACCEPTANCE IN THE LEDGER**: a row's acceptance cell must cite the spec's semantics, never re-quote a parameter list | `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## OPEN` row template | the `E4` row cites `docs/specs/relocate.md §…` after amendment |
| **K-6** | **`H-5` — ONE-HOP ESCALATION**: an ambiguity found at gate 1 is escalated to the architect IN THAT PASS (a required *open-semantics* field in the gate-1 record) | the gate-1 record shape | the two-hop deferral becomes impossible by construction |
| **K-7** | **`H-6` — A LAYER CLAUSE IN EVERY ADOPTION**: the dossier must state per behaviour whether any in-repo instrument can prove it, and the spec must carry the three-part refusal where none can | `AGENTS.md` item 5 + `gutter.md` ruling 11's precedent | `E4`'s ghost/expansion behaviours are declared `[U]`-only **at filing time**, not at gate 6 |
| **K-8** | **`H-7` — THE DECISIONS LINKAGE**: an architect ruling answering an identifier's meaning is recorded as a `docs/decisions.md` ACTIVE row **and referenced from the unit's ledger row, spec and dossier** | the archival loop (item 6) | `U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE` is reachable from the `E4` row and the spec |
| **K-9** | **`H-8` — A GLOSSARY IN THE FORK-FACING DOC** for every adopted name a fork must implement | `docs/FORKER.md` | the relocate parameter/seam glossary appears beside the seam-contract block `E10`'s ruling already requires there |

**WHAT THE FIX MUST *NOT* DO, recorded so it is not over-applied:** the bans stay (`threshold`'s presence on `P-5`/`R-1`/`S-11` is correct for the layers it guards — the fix is a **reconciliation** step, never a relaxation); the two gate-1 reviewers' split was **healthy** (it is the signal that the records were ambiguous, not a reason to merge the passes); and **`A-d4`'s adoption, the unit set and every landed contract are untouched** — the RCA asks for a **pre-filing** guard only.

## §L. ### L-1. THE ONE OPEN ARCHITECT QUESTION (the blocker; `AGENTS.md` item 10a's return point 2) — **⟶ RULED 2026-09-27, see the banner below; ⟶ DISCHARGED BY THE UNIT'S CLOSE-OUT 2026-09-27 (the gate-10 pass): the ruling's mapping was CONFIRMED at the spec gate, the contract's `P-RL-SM-1` carries the ruled terminal domain `{` `'end'` `}` and `P-RL-SM-5` the by-channel triple, the red set drives both, and `U-RELOCATE` IS `DONE`. Nothing in this subsection is open; it is kept as the QUESTION'S OWN RECORD, per this file's convention.**

**⟶ RULED 2026-09-27 — THE BLOCKER IS ANSWERED; THIS SUBSECTION IS KEPT AS THE QUESTION'S OWN RECORD.** **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim: *"The revealed zone hides again if the pane would not place into its zone. This can be because it was pulled back out of the threshold proximity, or because it has shifted to place into a different zone"*** — recorded as `docs/decisions.md`'s ACTIVE row **`U-RELOCATE-REVEALED-ZONE-HIDES-AGAIN`**, which carries the three clauses it settles and the derived mapping. **WHAT IT MEANS FOR THE TWO READINGS BELOW: the answer is (α)'s SHAPE PLUS a rule the question did not contain — the zone's displayed-ness is NOT MONOTONIC within a gesture (it turns off mid-drag on leaving proximity OR on retargeting to a different zone), so the mid-drag hide rides the PER-MOVE channel (B — the ghost's channel) and `onReveal` (channel A) stays the once-per-gesture terminal write, firing only at `'end'`.** **STILL OWED: the mapping *"the hide rides (B), therefore (A) reads ZERO on the invalid arm"* is a DERIVATION from the ruling + the ledger's *"exactly once per gesture at gesture end"* strengthening, and it is flagged for CONFIRMATION AT THE `E4` SPEC GATE** (the alternative — the hide being itself an `onReveal` write, which would make that strengthening FALSE AS WRITTEN — is recorded as architect-reversible in the decisions row).

**THE QUESTION, ONE SENTENCE: *"Does `onReveal` fire at every committing terminal, or only at `'end'`?"*** — **this is the residue the `threshold` ruling leaves open** (it settles the referent, the three channels and the reset arm's commit, and does **not** name `onReveal`'s committing-terminal set). **Owner: the architect. It must be ruled BEFORE `docs/specs/relocate.md` is filed**, because the spec's `§3` reveal clause and `P-RL-SM-1`'s declared terminal domain — and therefore that row's printed DRIVE term — are unwritable without it, and the two readings produce **different red sets**.

| Reading | What it pins | What it costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **(α) — RECOMMENDED** | `onReveal` fires **only on the `'end'` terminal**; the invalid arm's `reset` commits **exactly one** caller-supplied pre-drag value and writes **ZERO** reveals, the **visible revert** being carried by the **PREVIEW** channel — the shape the ruling itself cites and the shape `U-GUTTER-UI` landed (`gutter-ui.md` `§R.2` `R7`/`R8`(d), `§3.1` `M-13`) | The row's headline wording *"at gesture end"* stays **literally true**; the spec must additionally pin **which channel carries the invalid arm's revert** (else a `[T]` reader cannot tell) |
| **(β)** | `onReveal` fires at **every** committing terminal; the invalid arm writes the **pre-drag** reveal state back at `reset` | The module must hold and publish a **SECOND caller-supplied state** across the gesture; *"exactly once … at gesture end"* becomes **false as written** on the invalid arm; `P-RL-SM-4`'s retention domain grows by one value |

**Falsification of the *question's* status (recorded so it is not re-litigated):** the classification was **tested, not accepted** — the two readings are each derived from filed records, neither contradicts the other, and the `§6` DO-NOT list's settled `threshold` semantics are cited, **not questioned**.
